Well, um... I suppose it might seem like a bit much if you laid it all out at once. But it's not like I'm sitting down to a feast, you understand? 

Sometimes, well, I might start with some leftovers from dinner. And then, you know, I might make a sandwich or two... and perhaps have a bowl of cereal. And then maybe some cookies, or whatever snack foods happen to be sitting in the pantry. 

When you add it all up, I suppose... well, I guess it is more than I'd ever put on a plate for a normal supper. But it just sort of happens gradually, you know? It doesn't feel like I'm eating a massive amount all at once. I'm just having a little bit of this and a little bit of that. 

I hope that doesn't sound too terrible? I'm just trying to be honest with you about how it goes. Is that the kind of information you need?

S049\n  name: Eating_Large_Amount\n  description: Eating, in a discrete period of time, an amount of food that is definitely larger than what most individuals would eat in a similar period of time under similar circumstances.\n  manifestation: Some evenings I’ll start with leftovers and end up eating sandwiches, cereal, cookies, and whatever snack food is in the pantry, far more than I would ever put on a plate for a normal supper.\n- key: S050\n  name: Loss_of_Control\n  description: A sense of lack of control over eating during the episode (e.g., a feeling that one cannot stop eating or control what or how much one is eating).\n  manifestation: What brings me in is that once I start eating at night, it feels like I can see myself doing it and telling myself to stop, but my hands just keep going back for more.\n- key: additional_requirement_1\n  name: Additional Requirement 1\n  description: The binge eating and inappropriate compensatory behaviors both occur, on average, at least once a week for 3 months\n  manifestation: For the last several months, most weeks have had at least one night where I eat like that and then spend the next day trying to make up for it by skipping meals, setting strict food rules, or taking an extra-long walk.\n- key: functional_impairment\n  name: Functional Impairment\n  manifestation: I’m still managing my job and basic responsibilities, but I’ve been turning down theater and walking-club plans because I feel ashamed and preoccupied with whether I look disciplined enough.\n- key: duration\n  name: Duration\n  description: 4 months\n  manifestation: This has been going on for about 4 months, since my friend died and my evenings stopped feeling organized.\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 60 / architectural_or_engineering_manager / high_school\n- chief_complaint_symptom_code: S050\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Do you usually feel physically hungry when you start eating during these evening episodes, or do you sometimes start eating even when you're not really hungry?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose I must be at least a little hungry when I start. I mean, I usually work up quite an appetite during the day, dealing with the contractors and looking over the site plans and all that. \n\nI haven't really stopped to, you know, measure exactly how empty my stomach is beforehand. But I certainly wouldn't say I just eat for absolutely no reason at all. That would be rather... well, a bit gluttonous, wouldn't it? I just feel a bit peckish and decide to have a normal evening snack. \n\nI hope that makes sense? I'm sorry I can't be more precise about my exact hunger levels. I just assume it's a regular appetite. Is that... is that the sort of thing you were asking?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself eating until you feel uncomfortably full or even to the point of physical discomfort during these evening episodes?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness no, I wouldn't say uncomfortably full. I mean, maybe just pleasantly satisfied, like anyone would be after a good meal or a nice snack. \n\nI certainly don't gorge myself until I'm in pain or anything drastic like that. I just eat until I feel... well, until I feel done, I suppose. Sometimes I might feel a little heavy if I've had something rich, but it's nothing out of the ordinary. \n\nI try to be sensible about it, you know? I hope that's what you were looking to hear. I'm not doing anything too extreme, am I?\n\nDoctor: During these evening eating episodes, do you ever eat a much larger amount of food than most people would eat in a short period of time, like within a couple of hours?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I suppose it might seem like a bit much if you laid it all out at once. But it's not like I'm sitting down to a feast, you understand? \n\nSometimes, well, I might start with some leftovers from dinner. And then, you know, I might make a sandwich or two... and perhaps have a bowl of cereal. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... no, I don't think so. I mean, I certainly don't sneak around my own house or anything like that. That would be a bit... well, a bit unseemly for a man my age, wouldn't it? 

I usually just have my snacks right there in the kitchen or the den. I suppose my wife might be in the other room reading or watching television, so she's not always standing right over me, but I'm definitely not deliberately hiding it from her. I wouldn't want you to think I'm being deceitful or sneaking food into the garage or anything drastic. 

I just eat openly, like anyone else winding down for the evening. I mean, I might not announce every time I go to the pantry, but it's not a secret. Does that... does that sound alright to you? I really do try to keep things above board.

But I haven't really noticed if I keep going until it actually hurts or anything like that. I try not to let it get *that* out of hand, if that makes sense. \n\nI guess I just sort of realize I've had too much and eventually wrap things up. I'm sorry, I haven't really paid very close attention to exactly how my stomach feels right in the middle of it all. I hope that's a helpful answer? Did I understand your question correctly?\n\nDoctor: When you're eating during these evenings, do you find yourself eating much more quickly than usual?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I wouldn't say that. I mean, I try to just eat at my regular pace, I suppose. I don't really rush through it or shovel it in, if that's what you mean. \n\nI like to... well, just take my time, even when I'm, you know, having a bit too much. I hope that makes sense? I'm not trying to be difficult, I just haven't really noticed myself eating any faster than I would at a normal dinner. Is that... is that alright? I hope I'm giving you the right kind of information.\n\nDoctor: Before these evening eating episodes happen, do you usually feel stressed, anxious, sad, or upset?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't believe so. I mean, my life is really quite fine. Work can be busy, naturally, managing the projects and the team and all, but I wouldn't say I feel overly stressed or sad when I get home. I'm usually just... well, just having a regular, quiet evening, you know? \n\nI certainly don't feel upset or anxious before I start snacking. I hope that doesn't make my habit seem even more foolish... I know some folks eat when they're feeling down, but I think I just sort of... start eating without a very good excuse. \n\nI'm sorry, I hope that's an alright answer? I don't want to mislead you, I just haven't noticed any bad moods like that beforehand. Am I giving you the kind of information you need?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel ashamed or guilty about how much you eat during these evenings, or do you usually eat this way when you're alone?\n\nPatient: Well, um... I wouldn't say I feel *ashamed*, really. I mean, it's just a bit of extra snacking, isn't it? Nothing to be terribly guilty over, I don't think. I suppose I do prefer to have my evenings to myself, just to unwind and relax in my own space. \n\nMy wife is usually out with her book club or visiting our daughter on those nights anyway, so it just works out that I'm on my own. It's not like I'm hiding it or anything like that, it's just... well, it's just how the schedule falls. \n\nI hope that doesn't sound like I'm being sneaky? I just enjoy a quiet house and a bit of a treat, you know? I really hope I'm answering this the right way for you... is that the sort of detail you were looking for?\n\nDoctor: When you're having these evenings where you eat more than you plan, does it happen all in one sitting or over a long stretch of time?\n\nPatient: Oh, well, let me think... I suppose it's more spread out, really. Over a longer stretch, I mean. I might have a little something when I first sit down, and then maybe a bit more later on while I'm looking over some blueprints or just watching a program. \n\nIt's certainly not all at once in one big sitting—I wouldn't do that. It's just... grazing, I guess you'd call it? Just over the course of the night. I haven't really kept a strict eye on the clock, to be perfectly honest, so I can't give you an exact timeframe. \n\nI hope that's a satisfactory answer for you? I'm trying my best to remember how it usually goes. Well, um... I wouldn't say it's caused any *real* distress, not in a major way. I mean, I'm still going to work every day, managing my team at the firm just fine. I haven't let my basic responsibilities slip, you know? I'm very diligent about all of that.

People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, um... I suppose it's mostly over a couple of hours, really. Usually right after supper and before I turn in for the night. 

I mean, I don't want to make it sound like I'm completely gorging myself or anything terribly dramatic. It's just... well, some evenings I'll start by finishing off a bit of leftovers, you know, just tidying up the kitchen. But then I might make a sandwich or two, and maybe have a bowl of cereal... and then perhaps some cookies or whatever snack foods are sitting there in the pantry. 

When I look back on it, I suppose it ends up being far more than I would ever put on a plate for a normal, sit-down supper. It just sort of adds up quickly in that short window of time. 

I feel a bit foolish laying it all out like that. I hope you don't think I'm completely out of control or anything. It's just a bad habit I've fallen into, really. Does that answer your question alright? I want to make sure I'm giving you the right sort of details.

Well, um... I wouldn't say I'm eating frantically or anything like that. I'm certainly not shoveling food in, you know? I try to keep things quite civilized. 

I suppose... well, maybe I do eat a bit quicker than I would if I were sitting down at a nice restaurant with my wife. When you're just standing in the kitchen, tidying up and snacking, it's easy to just sort of... take bites a little faster, without really pausing to chat or enjoy the atmosphere. 

But I certainly wouldn't call it rapid or anything alarming. Just a bit more hurried than a proper sit-down meal, perhaps. Is that the sort of thing you're asking about? I just want to be sure I'm not making it sound worse than it actually is.
